Could use projected gradient on objective score. Let's design:
Score(p) = max_k c_k (with normalized sum) times 2n. We can compute active set K where c_k > M - delta. Gradient of c_k wrt p_j = p_{k-j}+p_{j-k}? Wait c_k = ∑_i p_i p_{k-i}. Derivative wrt p_j includes term i=j: p_{k-j}; and term i=k-j if different: p_j. So gradient = p_{k-j} (if 0≤k-j<n) + (if k-j ≠ j) p_j. More generally (∇c_k)_j = 1_{0≤k-j<n} p_{k-j} + 1_{0≤j-k<n} p_{j-k}. Since c_k quadratic symmetric, both terms. For k=0, c0=p0^2 derivative 2p0 (formula gives p0+p0). For active set, approximate gradient = average over active. Then perform multiplicative updates? Because p≥0. We can use exponentiated gradient: update p ← p * exp(-η g) then project/normalize. Need implement within 1800s and reliable. Let's detail each component.

Fast convolution scoring:
```
def _fft_linear_conv(p):
    n=len(p)
    N=1
    while N < 2*n-1: N <<=1
    F=np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(F*F, N)  # because real p, p*p
    return c[:2*n-1]
```
But (F*F) not |F|^2? For convolution of real p with itself, DFT of convolution is P^2 (ordinary product), not |P|^2. Since P may be complex; P^2 not equal |P|^2. Wait linear convolution theorem: FFT(conv(a,b)) = FFT(a)*FFT(b). For a=b real, it's P*P = P^2, not |P|^2. That can be complex? But result real; due to symmetry P conjugate, P^2 symmetric. If we use F*F (elementwise square), irfft works. If we used |F|^2, that's circular autocorrelation, not convolution. Important! For polynomial square, use P**2. For nonnegative p, c nonnegative but P^2 can have complex. Use `F * F` (not abs squared). Good catch. The circular convolution of p with itself uses P^2. Linear with zero padding. So fast_score uses square of DFT, not magnitude squared. This can produce small negative values due to numerical; clip at 0 for max? Since true nonnegative, use max(c) and maybe abs? If numerical negative at some positions, max affected only if all negative impossible. Use `np.max(c)` after clip small? Use `c = np.where(c < 0, 0, c)` for stable. But if numerical error positive tiny? ok.

Double-check: p real length n padded N. Linear convolution c = ifft(fft(p)*fft(p)). Yes.

Normalization: p sum=1. We should ensure p nonnegative. score=2*n*np.max(c). Because sum=1. If not normalize, score=2n max/sum^2.

Continuous formula uses convolution of density f, i.e. C(t)=∫ f(x)f(t-x) dx, which corresponds to P? It is linear convolution continuous, okay.

Full local search methods:
Method 1: Multiplicative updates based on c.
For p normalized, c = conv. Let M=np.max(c). Choose weights w_k = np.maximum(0, c_k - (1 - ε)*M) normalized? We want update p to reduce weighted sum maybe. If we update p_j ← p_j * exp(-η g_j), where g_j = ∑_k w_k (p_{k-j}+p_{j-k}). This reduces weighted active conv. We can compute g efficiently via FFT? g = correlation of w with p plus reversed. Let's derive: first term h1_j = ∑_k w_k p_{k-j} (valid). This is cross-correlation; if define reversed w? We can compute with FFT by padding. For all j, h1_j = ∑_{k} w_k p_{k-j}. Let indices 0..2n-2 for w, 0..n-1 p. This is convolution of reversed w with p? Define W_rev[t]=w_{L-1-t} for length L=2n-1. Then (W_rev * p)_m = ∑_t W_rev[t] p_{m-t} = ∑_t w_{L-1-t} p_{m-t}. best_p = p.copy(); best_s = fast_score(best_p)

    if time exceeded break
    cand = make_perturbation(best_p, rng, step)
    s = fast_score(cand)
    if s < best_s: accept, adapt step*=1.02
    else: step*=0.9
```

We can define `_score_theta(theta, n, basis)`:
```
f = np.ones(n)
for m in range(H):
   f += theta[2m]*cos[m] + theta[2m+1]*sin[m]
f = np.clip(f,0,None)
s=f.sum()
if s < 1e-6: return inf
p=f/s
return fast_score(p)
```

```
def conv_w_p(w,p):
   N = next_pow2(3*n)
   FW=np.fft.rfft(w,N)
   Fp=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
   return np.fft.irfft(FW*Fp,N)[:3*n-3]
```

def _gradient_candidate(p, rng, ampl=1.0, tau_frac=0.1, active_power=1):

    # weights

Let's detail parameter search implementation:
```
def _param_search(n, H, budget_until):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(123)
    i=np.arange(n)
    cos_sins = []
    for m in range(1,H+1):
       ang=m*np.pi*i/n
       cos_sins.append((np.cos(ang), np.sin(ang)))
    theta=np.zeros(2*H)
    def eval_theta(theta): ... (top-level requires basis,n? Could pass via global? We can define function with parameters but no closures. In propose, we can't define nested? Rules: Make all helper functions top level and have no closures. So cannot define eval_theta inside propose. We can define top-level `_score_theta(theta, n, basis)` where basis list of arrays.
```
But passing basis list each call overhead. Fine.

For grid, we can call `_score_theta` many times; each builds p from scratch looping H. H maybe 4, okay. But for first grid only 2 dims, we have faster `_score_2param(a,b,cos1,sin1,n)`.
Top-level:
```
def _score_2param(a,b,c1,s1,n):
   f=1.0 + a*c1 + b*s1
   f=np.where(f>0,f,0)
   s=f.sum()
   if s<1e-12: return inf
   p=f/s
   return _fast_score_norm(p,n)
```

Implement gradient candidate function top-level:
```
def _gradient_perturb(p, c, rng, K=15, etas=(0.05,0.1,0.25,0.5)):
    n=len(p)
    M=np.max(c)
    if M<=0: return None
    # choose active indices: top K with random jitter
    K = min(K, len(c))
    # sample indices proportional to c^4 maybe
    weights = c**4
    wsum=weights.sum()
    if wsum<=0: idx=np.argmax(c); idxs=[idx]
    else:
       probs=weights/wsum
       idxs=np.unique(rng.choice(len(c), size=K, replace=False, p=probs))
    g=np.zeros(n)
    for k in idxs:
       lo=max(0,k-(n-1)); hi=min(k,n-1)
       if lo>hi: continue
       i=np.arange(lo,hi+1)
       g[i] += p[k-i] + p[i]
    gmax=np.max(g)
    if gmax<=0: return None
    g/=gmax
    logp=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300))
    best_p=None; best_s=inf
    for eta in etas:
       lp=logp - eta*g
       lp=np.clip(lp, -50, 50)
       cand=np.exp(lp); cand/=cand.sum()
       s=_fast_score_norm(cand,n)
       if s<best_s: best_s=s; best_p=cand
    return best_p,best_s
```
Sampling top indices with probability c^4 works but advantage? For very flat c, uniform-ish. Could use top K exactly: `idxs = np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]`. Simpler and targets max. Maybe randomize by adding noise to c: `c_noisy = c + rng.normal(0,0.01*M, len(c)); idxs=argpartition...`. Good.

Block perturbation:
```
def _block_perturb(p, rng, n, sigma=0.1):
   logp=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300))
   num_blocks = 1 + (rng.random()<0.3)
   lp=logp.copy()
   for _ in range(num_blocks):
      width = int(rng.integers(max(2,n//200), max(3,n//8)))
      start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1,n-width)))
      lp[start:start+width] += rng.normal(0, sigma)
   # maybe optional smooth long block
   cand=np.exp(np.clip(lp,-50,50)); cand/=cand.sum()
   return cand
```
Use adaptive sigma. Gradient + block alternate.

```
p = np.asarray(p,float)
p = np.clip(p,0,1000)
s=p.sum()
if s<1e-12: return inf, p
p_norm=p/s
N=1
while N < 2*n-1: N <<=1
F=np.fft.rfft(p_norm,N)
c=np.fft.irfft(F*F,N, n=2*n-1? irfft parameter n specifies output length. Use `np.fft.irfft(F*F, N)[:2*n-1]`.
c=np.maximum(c,0)
maxc=c.max()
score=2*n*maxc  # since sum=1
return float(score), c
```

Full gradient candidate function:
```
def _softmax_gradient_candidate(p, c, M, rng):
    n=len(p)
    L=2*n-1
    alpha = rng.choice(alphas)/M  # alphas [5,10,20,50]
    logits=alpha*(c-M)
    w=np.exp(logits); wsum=w.sum(); w/=wsum
    # compute grad
    Mfft=1
    while Mfft < 3*n-3: Mfft <<=1
    Fw=np.fft.rfft(w, Mfft)
    Fp=np.fft.rfft(p, Mfft)
    Fpr=np.fft.rfft(p[::-1], Mfft)
    C1=np.fft.irfft(Fw*Fpr, Mfft)
    C2=np.fft.irfft(Fw*Fp, Mfft)
    g = C1[n-1:2*n-1] + C2[:n]
    # numerical cleanup
    g=np.maximum(g,0)  # gradient of nonnegative weighted quadratic should nonneg? w,p nonneg => derivative nonneg, FFT may produce tiny neg. clip.
    gmax=np.max(g)
    if gmax<=0: return None
    g/=gmax
    logp=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300))
    # try eta
    best=None
    for eta in [0.05,0.1,0.25,0.5,1.0]:
        lp=logp - eta*g
        lp=np.clip(lp, -40, 40)
        cand=np.exp(lp); cand/=cand.sum()
        s,cc=_fast_score_and_conv(cand,n)
        if s<best: ...
    return best
```

Now code implementation details.

Top-level helpers (no closures/lambdas):
```
import numpy as np, time, math

def _next_pow2(m):
    n=1
    while n<m: n <<=1
    return n

def _fast_score_and_conv(p, n):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = p.sum()
    if s < 1e-300:
        return np.inf, np.zeros(2*n-1)
    p = p / s
    N = _next_pow2(2*n-1)
    F = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F*F, N)
    c = c[:2*n-1]
    # remove tiny negatives
    c[~np.isfinite(c)] = 0.0
    if np.any(c < 0):
        c = np.clip(c, 0, None)
    maxc = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    score = 2.0 * float(n) * maxc
    return score, c

def _fast_score(p, n):
    s,c=_fast_score_and_conv(p,n)
    return s
```
This normalizes and returns normalized c.

Note: `np.fft.irfft(F*F, N)` output length N; if N >2n-1, we slice. But linear convolution length 2n-1; zero padding no wrap. Fine.

Parameter base:
```
def _make_power_n(base_beta, alpha, n, flip=False):
    i=np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if abs(beta)<1e-12:
       base=np.ones(n)
    else:
       base=np.exp(-beta*np.log(i+alpha))
    if flip: base=base[::-1]
    s=base.sum()
    return base/s
```
Power law can have base[0] huge if alpha small, but normalized sum ~√n, p max ~1/√n, okay.

Modulation score:
```
def _score_modulation(theta, base, basis, n):
    f = base.copy()
    for idx in range(0, len(theta), 2):
       c=basis[idx//2][0]; s=basis[idx//2][1]
       f += base*(theta[idx]*c + theta[idx+1]*s)
    # Instead of base.copy + terms; compute mod then multiply to avoid large? base may large; fine.
    np.maximum(f, 0, out=f)
    s=f.sum()
    if s<1e-300: return np.inf
    f/=s
    sc,c=_fast_score_and_conv(f,n)
    return sc
```
But if base is normalized already, multiplying by mod changes sum; okay. Basing on normalized base. Use `f = base * (1 + mod)` for numerical. In loop, `mod = np.zeros(n)` then add theta*cos; `f = base*(1+mod)`. This allocates. Fine.

For full p local search, maintain logp? Maybe directly p. For block perturb, log.

Local search function? Need top-level? We can implement inside `propose_candidate` loop without nested function. But rules: helper functions top-level no closures. The main can have loops and call top-level candidate generators.

We can define top-level `_apply_log_update(logp, g, eta, clip_low=-40, clip_high=40)`:
```
def _log_update_candidate(logp, g, eta):
    lp = logp - eta*g
    np.clip(lp, -40,40, out=lp)
    p=np.exp(lp)
    p/=p.sum()
    return p
```
But g length n.

Gradient top active:
```
def _top_active_gradient(c, p, rng, K):
    n=len(p)
    L=len(c)
    # add noise to c for randomization
    M = max(c.max(), 1e-300)
    c_noisy = c + rng.normal(0.0, 0.05*M, L)
    c_noisy = np.maximum(c_noisy, 0)
    if K >= L:
       idx = np.arange(L)
    else:
       idx = np.argpartition(c_noisy, L-K)[L-K:]
    g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for k in idx:
       k=int(k)
       lo = max(0, k-(n-1))
       hi = min(k, n-1)
       if lo > hi: continue
       i = np.arange(lo, hi+1)
       g[i] += p[k-i] + p[i]
    g = np.maximum(g, 0)
    gmax = g.max()
    if gmax > 0: g /= gmax
    return g
```
DataFrame: `p[k-i]` where k-i array, valid by range.

Full softmax gradient maybe too heavy; we can implement optionally for a few iterations if time. But top gradient likely okay.

Block perturb:
```
def _block_candidate(logp, rng, sigma, n):
    lp = logp.copy()
    num = int(rng.integers(1,3))
    for _ in range(num):
       width = int(rng.integers(max(2,n//200), max(3,n//4)))
       start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1,n-width)))
       lp[start:start+width] += rng.normal(0.0, sigma)
    np.clip(lp, -40,40, out=lp)
    p=np.exp(lp); p/=p.sum()
    return p
```
We can also add global scale? Normalization handles.

def _local_search(p, n, rng, deadline):
    score,c = _fast_score_and_conv(p,n)
    best_p=p.copy(); best_score=score
    logp = np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300))
    sigma=0.15
    eta=0.1
    T=0.02
    it=0
    n_iter_target = 3000?
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
       it+=1
       r = rng.random()
       candidate=None; cand_score=inf; cand_c=None
       if r < 0.55:
          g = _top_active_gradient(c,p,rng,K=10+int(rng.integers(0,20)))
          if g is not None:
             # maybe try eta and eta/2
             for mult in [1.0,0.5]:
                 e=eta*mult
                 cand=_log_update(logp,g,e)
                 sc,cc=_fast_score_and_conv(cand,n)
                 if sc<cand_score: ...
       else:
          cand=_block_candidate(logp,rng,sigma,n)
          sc,cc=...
       # accept
       if cand_score < score or rng.random() < math.exp((score-cand_score)/T) if cand_score finite:
           p=cand; score=cand_score; c=cand_c; logp=np.log(max(p,1e-300))
       if score < best_score:
           best_p=p.copy(); best_score=score
       # adapt
       if accepted improve: sigma*=1.02; eta*=1.05; T*=0.99
       else: sigma*=0.97; eta*=0.95
       # bounds
       sigma=np.clip(sigma,0.01,0.8)
       eta=np.clip(eta,0.01,3.0)
       T=max(T,1e-5)
    return best_p,best_score
```

Full gradient function (top-level):
```
def _full_softmax_gradient(c, p, alpha_factor, n):
    M=max(c.max(),1e-300)
    logits=(c-M)/M*alpha_factor  # alpha_factor dimensionless e.g. 20
    w=np.exp(logits); wsum=w.sum(); if wsum<=0: w=delta
    w/=wsum
    N=_next_pow2(3*n-3)
    Fw=np.fft.rfft(w,N)
    Fp=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    Fpr=np.fft.rfft(p[::-1],N)
    C1=np.fft.irfft(Fw*Fpr,N)
    C2=np.fft.irfft(Fw*Fp,N)
    g=C1[n-1:2*n-1] + C2[:n]
    g=np.maximum(g,0)
    gmax=g.max(); if gmax>0: g/=gmax
    return g
```
Use alpha_factor 10 or 30. This emphasizes all c via softmax; if alpha=10/M, weights ~ exp(10*(c-M)/M). Many with c near M. Good. Cost higher. We can use in local search mode if rng <0.2 or after stagnant.

Need be careful `N <=?` For n=8192, 3n-3=24573, N=32768. `C1` length 3n-3. Access slice n-1:2n-1 (length n). Good. `C2[:n]`.

Could combine with top active for speed. Good.

Pseudo code `propose_candidate`:
```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    t0=time.monotonic()
    budget=max(60,float(budget_s))
    deadline=t0+budget-5
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    n=8192 if budget>900 else 4096
    print(f"Using n={n} budget={budget_s}")
    # basis for trig m=1..H
    H=4
    idx=np.arange(n,dtype=float)
    basis=[]
    for m in range(1,H+1):
       ang=m*np.pi*idx/n
       basis.append((np.cos(ang), np.sin(ang)))
    # candidate pool list for exact maybe
    candidates=[]
    # uniform
    uniform=np.ones(n)/n
    s,c=_fast_score_and_conv(uniform,n)
    candidates.append((s,uniform))
    # power laws grid
    betas=[0.4,0.45,0.48,0.5,0.52,0.55,0.6]
    alphas=[0.5,1.0,1.5,2.0]
    best_bases=[]
    for beta in betas:
       for alpha in alphas:
          i=np.arange(n,float)
          base=np.exp(-beta*np.log(i+alpha))
          p=base/base.sum()
          s,c=_fast_score_and_conv(p,n)
          candidates.append((s,p.copy()))
          # maybe store base for modulation: store normalized base and beta,alpha
          if s < best_bases... keep top 3? 
    # select top 2 bases by pure score. But for modulation, use base (not p?). p is normalized base.
```
For modulation, if we use normalized base, score_mod with base normalized and mod. Good.

best_p = candidates[0][1]; best_score = candidates[0][0]
for base in top_bases[:2]:
    # theta search
    theta=np.zeros(2*H)
    sc, _ = _score_modulation(theta, base, basis, n) # should equal base score maybe slight
    best_theta=theta.copy(); best_mo=sc
    steps=[0.25,0.125,0.0625,0.03125]
    step=0.25
    for _cycle in range(4):
       # coordinate
       for d in range(2*H):
          for sign in (1,-1):
             trial=best_theta.copy(); trial[d]+=sign*step
             sc=_score_modulation(trial,base,basis,n)

       # random

          trial=best_theta + rng.normal(0,step,2*H)
          sc=_score_modulation(trial,base,basis,n)
          if sc < best_mo:
             best_theta=trial; best_mo=sc; ...
       step*=0.5
       if time exceeded: break
    p = _mod_to_p(best_theta, base, basis, n)
    s,c=_fast_score_and_conv(p,n)

    candidates.append((s,p))

Local search:
```
# choose start p = best fast candidate
p=best_p.copy()
score,c=_fast_score_and_conv(p,n)
# ensure best_exact maybe initialize with uniform? We'll exact at end.
local_deadline=deadline-90
best_local_p=p.copy(); best_local_score=score

while time.monotonic()<local_deadline:
   it+=1
   cand=None; cand_score=inf; cand_c=None; mode=0
   if it % 7 == 0: # full gradient
      g=_full_softmax_gradient(c,p,20.0,n)
      if g is not None:
         e=eta
         cand=_log_update(logp,g,e)
         cand_score,cand_c=_fast_score_and_conv(cand,n)
   else:
      mode=rng.random()
      if mode<0.6:
         K=int(rng.integers(5,30))
         g=_top_active_gradient(c,p,rng,K)
         if g is not None:
            e=eta

            cand_score,cand_c=_fast_score_and_conv(cand,n)
            if cand_score >= score and rng.random()<0.3:
                 cand2=_log_update(logp,g,max(0.01,e*0.5))
                 sc2,cc2=_fast_score_and_conv(cand2,n)
                 if sc2<cand_score: cand=cand2;cand_score=sc2;cand_c=cc2
      else:
         cand=_block_candidate(logp,rng,sigma,n)
         cand_score,cand_c=_fast_score_and_conv(cand,n)
   if np.isfinite(cand_score):
      accept = cand_score < score
      if not accept:
          delta=cand_score-score
          if delta < 5*T:
             accept = rng.random() < math.exp(-delta/T)
      if accept:
          p=cand; score=cand_score; c=cand_c
          logp=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300))
          if score < best_local_score:
              best_local_score=score; best_local_p=p.copy(); candidates.append((score,p.copy())); no_improve=0
              if score < target: print progress
          else: no_improve+=1
      else:
          no_improve +=1
      # adapt
      if accept:
         sigma=np.clip(sigma*1.03,0.02,0.8); eta=np.clip(eta*1.05,0.02,3.0); T=max(T*0.99,1e-6)
      else:
         sigma=np.clip(sigma*0.97,0.02,0.8); eta=np.clip(eta*0.97,0.02,3.0)
   if no_improve > 300:
      # reset current to best local with small noise
      p=best_local_p.copy(); score=best_local_score; c=_fast_score_and_conv(p,n)[1]; logp=np.log(max(p,1e-300)); no_improve=0; sigma=max(0.05,sigma*0.5); eta=max(0.05,eta*0.5)
   # print progress every 500
   if it % 500==0: print(...)
```

Let's make top-level:
```
def _p_from_features(theta, base, features):
    mod = np.ones(n)
    for t,col in zip(theta, features): mod += t*col
    f = base*mod
    return np.maximum(f,0)
```

features_array = np.vstack((z, z*z, z*z*z, np.sin(2πz), np.cos(2πz), np.sin(πz), np.cos(πz), np.sin(4πz)))

    theta = np.zeros(D)
    best_theta=theta.copy(); best_sc,_=_score_features(theta,base,features,n)
    # optional direct manual coefficients grid? coordinate.
    steps=[0.3,0.15,0.075]
    for step in steps:
       # coordinate
       for d in range(D):
          for sign in [1,-1]:
             trial=best_theta.copy(); trial[d]=np.clip(best_theta[d]+sign*step,-1,1)
             sc,_=_score_features(trial,base,features,n)
             if sc<best_sc: accept
       # random 300
       for _ in range(300):
          trial=np.clip(best_theta + rng.normal(0, step, D), -1.2,1.2)
          sc,_=_score_features(trial,base,features,n)
       step*=0.5
    cand = features_to_p
```

i=np.arange(n,float)
if beta==0: base=np.ones(n)
else: base=np.exp(-beta*np.log(i+alpha))
if flip: base=base[::-1]
base /= base.sum()
return base
```

Let's outline code in detail.

```
import numpy as np
import time
import math

_target = 1.5027

def _next_pow2(m): ...
def _fast_score_and_conv(p, n): ...
def _fast_score(p,n): ...
def _log_candidate(logp, g, eta): ...
def _top_gradient(c,p,rng,K): ...
def _full_gradient(c,p,alpha,n): ...
def _block_candidate(logp,rng,sigma,n): ...
def _make_base(beta,alpha,n,flip): ...
def _score_features(theta, base, features, n):
    mod=1.0 + np.dot(theta, features)
    f=base*mod
    f=np.maximum(f,0.0)
    s=f.sum()
    if s < 1e-300: return np.inf, None
    f=f/s
    return _fast_score_and_conv(f,n)
def _p_from_features(theta, base, features):
    mod=1+np.dot(theta, features)
    f=base*mod
    f=np.maximum(f,0)
    s=f.sum()
    return f/s

def _to_list(p): ...
```
In `_top_gradient`, creating `i=np.arange(lo,hi+1)` each active; could be heavy. We can optimize by using slice and vector operations without arange? Need p[k-i] where i range lo..hi. This is p slice `[k-hi : k-lo+1]` reversed? For i from lo to hi, j=k-i from k-lo down to k-hi. So p[j] = p[k-hi:k-lo+1][::-1]. We can do:
```
i_vals? g[lo:hi+1] += p[k-hi:k-lo+1][::-1] + p[lo:hi+1]
```
This avoids arange. Let's verify: length L=hi-lo+1. p_slice = p[k-hi : k-lo+1] gives j = k-hi,...,k-lo. Reversed gives j = k-lo,...,k-hi corresponding i=lo,...,hi. Good. Use `g[lo:hi+1] += p[k-hi:k-lo+1][::-1] + p[lo:hi+1]`.
This vectorized slice fast.

`_top_gradient` code:
```
def _top_gradient(c,p,rng,K):
    n=len(p); L=len(c)
    M=float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    if M <=0: return None
    c_noisy = c + rng.normal(0.0, 0.05*M, L)
    idx = np.argpartition(c_noisy, L-K)[L-K:] if K<L else np.arange(L)
    g=np.zeros(n)
    for k in idx:
       k=int(k)
       lo = 0 if k < n-1? actually max(0, k-(n-1)); hi = min(k,n-1)
       if lo>hi: continue
       g[lo:hi+1] += p[k-hi:k-lo+1][::-1] + p[lo:hi+1]
    g=np.maximum(g,0)
    gmax=g.max()
    if gmax>0: g/=gmax
    return g
```
For k near 0, hi=k, lo=0, p[k-hi:k-lo+1]=p[0:k+1] reversed. Good. For k near 2n-2, lo=k-n+1, hi=n-1, p[k-hi:k-lo+1]=p[k-(n-1):k-lo+1]; indices valid? k=2n-2, lo=n-1, hi=n-1, p[k-hi:n-1? k-lo+1 =n? p[0? wait k-hi=2n-2-(n-1)=n-1; k-lo+1=2n-2-(n-1)+1=n; p[n-1:n] ok. Good.

`_full_gradient`: ensure slices valid.
```
def _full_gradient(c,p,alpha,n):
   M=max(c.max(), tiny)
   logits=(c-M)/M*alpha
   w=np.exp(logits)
   wsum=w.sum()
   if not finite or <=0: return None
   w/=wsum
   N=_next_pow2(3*n-3)
   Fw=np.fft.rfft(w,N)
   Fp=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
   Fpr=np.fft.rfft(p[::-1],N)
   C1=np.fft.irfft(Fw*Fpr,N)
   C2=np.fft.irfft(Fw*Fp,N)
   g=C1[n-1:2*n-1] + C2[:n]
   g=np.maximum(g,0)
   gmax=g.max()
   if gmax>0: g/=gmax
   return g
```
Potential memory: C1,C2 length 32768 each, fine.

Local search `logp` initialization: If p has zeros, log(0) -inf; use `np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))`. Candidate log update clip [-35,35]? Use -40, positive. If p very small, adding g may not raise. Good.

Maybe we should use `lp = logp - eta * g` where g max1. If eta large, many p decrease, normalize increases others. Could create zeros. Clip.

Simulated annealing acceptance: Need avoid overflow in exp. If delta = cand_score - score > 0. `accept_prob = math.exp(-delta / T)` if delta/T<700 else 0. Use threshold.

Feature search implementation details:
```
def _optimize_features(base, features, n, rng, time_limit):
    D = len(features) # features is 2D? len gives D if 2D
    theta=np.zeros(D)
    best_theta=theta.copy()
    best_sc,_=_score_features(theta,base,features,n)
    end=time.monotonic()+time_limit
    steps=[0.3,0.15,0.075,0.0375]
    for step in steps:
       if time.monotonic()>end: break
       # coordinate multiple passes? one pass
       for d in range(D):
          for sign in (1.0,-1.0):
             trial=best_theta.copy()
             trial[d]=min(1.2,max(-1.2,trial[d]+sign*step))
             sc,_=_score_features(trial,base,features,n)
             if sc < best_sc:
                best_sc=sc; best_theta=trial.copy()
       # random
       n_random = 400 if step>0.1 else 200
       for _ in range(n_random):
          if time.monotonic()>end: break
          trial=np.clip(best_theta + rng.normal(0.0, step, D), -1.2,1.2)
          sc,_=_score_features(trial,base,features,n)
          if sc < best_sc:
             best_sc=sc; best_theta=trial.copy()
       # maybe also try one-shot Gaussian from zero
       for _ in range(50):
          trial=np.clip(rng.normal(0.0, step, D), -1.2,1.2)
          sc,_=...
    return best_theta,best_sc
```

For speed, use `np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)`. But c slice view; okay.

```
def _fast_score_and_conv(p,n):
    p=np.asarray(p,dtype=np.float64)
    if p.shape != (n,): p=p[:n]? assume.
    s=p.sum()
    if s<=1e-300 or not np.isfinite(s): return inf,zeros
    p = p / s
    length=2*n-1
    N=_next_pow2(length)
    F=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(F*F,N)
    c = c[:length]
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(c)):
       c=np.nan_to_num(c, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    np.maximum(c,0.0,out=c)
    maxc=float(np.max(c))
    return float(2*n*maxc), c
```
If p contains nan, s nan -> inf. We ensure finite.

In feature search, base finite. Good.

   arr=np.clip(arr,0,1000)
   if arr.size>0 and np.isfinite(arr).all():
       val=_exact_score_list(arr.tolist())
       if val < best_exact: best_exact=val; best_seq=arr.tolist()
```
If parent huge, arr.tolist maybe memory; okay.

uni_list = [1.0]*n
val=_exact_score_list(uni_list)
if val < best_exact: ...
```
But if n=12288, [1.0]*n okay.

Now, one more mathematical check: In `_fast_score_and_conv`, if p is not normalized by sum exactly 1 due to previous exp clip, we normalize. This means candidate's logp may not correspond to normalized p; but local accepts normalized p. Good.

In `_log_candidate`, after normalize, if all p underflow? exp clip [-40,40], sum positive. Good.

In local acceptance, if candidate score finite but `cand_c` sum normalized. Good.

If `cand_score` inf, skip.

Now, code final. Let's ensure docstring short.

```python
"""
Search for non-negative step-function heights minimizing
2*n*max(conv(a,a))/sum(a)^2.

Main ingredients:
- start from power-law bases and low-dimensional feature-modulated bases;
- refine with multiplicative gradient / random-log-block search;
- use FFT for internal scoring and exact evaluator for final candidates.
"""

import math
import time
import numpy as np

_TARGET = 1.5027


def _next_pow2(m):
    n = 1
    while n < m:
        n <<= 1
    return n


def _fast_score_and_conv(p, n):
    """Internal fast exact-scale evaluation using FFT for full linear convolution."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.size != n:
        p = p.reshape(-1)[:n]
    s = float(p.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-300:
        return float("inf"), np.zeros(2 * n - 1, dtype=np.float64)
    p = p / s
    length = 2 * n - 1
    N = _next_pow2(length)
    F = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, N)
    c = c[:length]
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(c)):
        c = np.nan_to_num(c, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)
    maxc = float(np.max(c))
    return float(2.0 * n * maxc), c


def _fast_score(p, n):
    s, _ = _fast_score_and_conv(p, n)
    return s


def _make_base(beta, alpha, n, flip=False):
    """Base sequence (i+alpha)^(-beta), normalized."""
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if abs(beta) < 1e-12:
        base = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        base = np.exp(-beta * np.log(i + alpha))
    if flip:
        base = base[::-1]
    s = float(base.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        base = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(n)
    return base / s


def _score_features(theta, base, features, n):
    """Score base * (1 + features @ theta), after nonnegative clipping/normalization."""
    theta = np.asarray(theta, dtype=np.float64)
    mod = 1.0 + np.dot(theta, features)
    f = base * mod
    f = np.maximum(f, 0.0)
    s = float(f.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-300:
        return float("inf"), None
    f = f / s
    return _fast_score_and_conv(f, n)


def _p_from_features(theta, base, features):
    theta = np.asarray(theta, dtype=np.float64)
    mod = 1.0 + np.dot(theta, features)
    f = base * mod
    f = np.maximum(f, 0.0)
    s = float(f.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return None
    return f / s


def _optimize_features(base, features, n, rng, time_limit):
    """Coordinate + random search in a small feature space."""
    D = int(features.shape[0])
    theta = np.zeros(D, dtype=np.float64)
    best_theta = theta.copy()
    best_sc, _ = _score_features(best_theta, base, features, n)

    end = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, time_limit)
    steps = [0.30, 0.15, 0.075, 0.0375]

    for step in steps:
        if time.monotonic() > end:
            break

        # Coordinate moves.
        for d in range(D):
            if time.monotonic() > end:
                break
            for sign in (1.0, -1.0):
                trial = best_theta.copy()
                trial[d] = min(1.25, max(-1.25, trial[d] + sign * step))
                sc, _ = _score_features(trial, base, features, n)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
                    best_sc = sc
                    best_theta = trial.copy()

        # Local random jitter.
        n_rand = 350 if step > 0.1 else 180
        for _ in range(n_rand):
            if time.monotonic() > end:
                break
            trial = np.clip(best_theta + rng.normal(0.0, step, D), -1.25, 1.25)
            sc, _ = _score_features(trial, base, features, n)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
                best_sc = sc
                best_theta = trial.copy()

        # Occasional restarts from zero to avoid small-basin traps.
        for _ in range(40):
            if time.monotonic() > end:
                break
            trial = np.clip(rng.normal(0.0, max(0.15, step * 1.4), D), -1.25, 1.25)
            sc, _ = _score_features(trial, base, features, n)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
                best_sc = sc
                best_theta = trial.copy()

    return best_theta, float(best_sc)


def _log_candidate(logp, g, eta):
    lp = logp - eta * g
    np.clip(lp, -40.0, 40.0, out=lp)
    p = np.exp(lp)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return None
    return p / s


def _top_active_gradient(c, p, rng, K):
    """Gradient of sum of K highest noisy convolution coefficients."""
    n = len(p)
    L = len(c)
    M = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    if M <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(M):
        return None
    c_noisy = c + rng.normal(0.0, 0.02 * M, L)
    c_noisy = np.maximum(c_noisy, 0.0)
    if K >= L:
        idx = np.arange(L)
    else:
        idx = np.argpartition(c_noisy, L - K)[L - K:]

    g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for kk in idx:
        k = int(kk)
        lo = max(0, k - (n - 1))
        hi = min(k, n - 1)
        if lo > hi:
            continue
        # derivative of c_k wrt p[lo:hi+1]
        g[lo:hi + 1] += p[k - hi:k - lo + 1][::-1] + p[lo:hi + 1]

    np.maximum(g, 0.0, out=g)
    gm = float(np.max(g))
    if gm <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(gm):
        return None
    return g / gm


def _full_softmax_gradient(c, p, alpha, n):
    """Smooth weighted gradient using softmax weights over all coefficients."""
    M = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    if M <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(M):
        return None
    logits = (c - M) / M * alpha
    w = np.exp(logits)
    ws = float(w.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(ws) or ws <= 0.0:
        return None
    w = w / ws

    length3 = 3 * n - 3
    N = _next_pow2(length3)
    Fw = np.fft.rfft(w, N)
    Fp = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    Fpr = np.fft.rfft(p[::-1], N)
    C1 = np.fft.irfft(Fw * Fpr, N)
    C2 = np.fft.irfft(Fw * Fp, N)
    g = C1[n - 1:2 * n - 1] + C2[:n]
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
        g = np.nan_to_num(g, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    np.maximum(g, 0.0, out=g)
    gm = float(np.max(g))
    if gm <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(gm):
        return None
    return g / gm


def _block_candidate(logp, rng, sigma, n):
    lp = logp.copy()
    nblocks = int(rng.integers(1, 3))
    for _ in range(nblocks):
        lo_w = max(2, n // 256)
        hi_w = max(lo_w + 1, n // 4)
        width = int(rng.integers(lo_w, hi_w + 1))
        start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
        lp[start:start + width] += rng.normal(0.0, sigma)
    np.clip(lp, -40.0, 40.0, out=lp)
    p = np.exp(lp)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return None
    return p / s


def _to_valid_list(p):
    arr = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    return [float(x) for x in arr]


def _exact_score(seq):
    try:
        return float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    """Return a non-negative sequence optimizing the evaluator."""
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    try:
        budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget = 1800.0
    budget = max(60.0, budget)
    final_deadline = t0 + budget - 4.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    if budget >= 700.0:
        n = 8192
    elif budget >= 300.0:
        n = 4096
    else:
        n = 2048

    print("Starting search with n =", n, flush=True)

    # Candidate pool: tuples (fast_score, unique_order, p_array)
    candidates = []
    order = 0

    def add_candidate(p):
        nonlocal order
        if p is None:
            return
        p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        if p.size != n:
            return
        sc, _ = _fast_score_and_conv(p, n)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            candidates.append((sc, order, p.copy()))
            order += 1

    # Feature matrix for low-dimensional modulation.
    z = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    features = np.vstack((
        z,
        z * z,
        z * z * z,
        np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * z),
        np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * z),
        np.sin(np.pi * z),
        np.cos(np.pi * z),
        np.sin(4.0 * np.pi * z),
    ))

    # Generate and score simple power-law bases.
    base_entries = []
    base_order = 0
    betas = [0.0, 0.42, 0.46, 0.48, 0.50, 0.52, 0.55, 0.60]
    alphas = [0.5, 0.75, 1.0, 1.5]
    for beta in betas:
        for alpha in alphas:
            for flip in (False, True):
                if beta == 0.0 and flip:
                    continue
                base = _make_base(beta, alpha, n, flip)
                sc, _ = _fast_score_and_conv(base, n)
                base_entries.append((sc, base_order, base))
                base_order += 1
                if sc < 1.9:
                    add_candidate(base)
    base_entries.sort()
    print("Best pure base fast score =", base_entries[0][0], flush=True)

    # Feature optimization for a few of the best bases.
    feature_deadline = t0 + min(420.0, max(60.0, 0.25 * (budget - 120.0)))
    top_bases = [b[2] for b in base_entries[:3]]
    for base in top_bases:
        if time.monotonic() > feature_deadline:
            break
        theta, sc = _optimize_features(base, features, n, rng, max(6.0, feature_deadline - time.monotonic()))
        p = _p_from_features(theta, base, features)
        if p is not None and np.isfinite(sc) and sc < 2.0:
            add_candidate(p)
            print("Feature candidate fast score =", sc, flush=True)

    if not candidates:
        uni = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        add_candidate(uni)

    candidates.sort()
    start_p = candidates[0][2].copy()
    best_fast = candidates[0][0]
    print("Best fast candidate before local search =", best_fast, flush=True)

    # Full local search in log-space.
    p = start_p.copy()
    cur_score, c = _fast_score_and_conv(p, n)
    best_local_p = p.copy()
    best_local_score = cur_score
    logp = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))

    sigma = 0.18
    eta = 0.12
    T = 0.004
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0
    local_deadline = final_deadline - 60.0

    while time.monotonic() < local_deadline:
        it += 1
        cand = None
        cand_score = float("inf")
        cand_c = None
        accepted = False

        # Occasionally use smooth full-gradient, otherwise active-set gradient or random block.
        use_full = (it % 7 == 0)
        if use_full:
            g = _full_softmax_gradient(c, p, 18.0, n)
        else:
            mode = rng.random()
            if mode < 0.62:
                K = int(rng.integers(8, 36))
                g = _top_active_gradient(c, p, rng, K)
            else:
                cand = _block_candidate(logp, rng, sigma, n)
                if cand is not None:
                    cand_score, cand_c = _fast_score_and_conv(cand, n)
                g = None

        if g is not None:
            best_e = eta
            best_sc = float("inf")
            best_cc = None
            best_pp = None
            etas = [eta, max(0.02, eta * 0.5)]
            if rng.random() < 0.25:
                etas.append(min(4.0, eta * 1.9))
            for e in etas:
                cand_i = _log_candidate(logp, g, e)
                if cand_i is None:
                    continue
                sc_i, cc_i = _fast_score_and_conv(cand_i, n)
                if np.isfinite(sc_i) and sc_i < best_sc:
                    best_sc = sc_i
                    best_cc = cc_i
                    best_pp = cand_i
                    best_e = e
            cand = best_pp
            cand_score = best_sc
            cand_c = best_cc
            eta = min(4.0, max(0.02, best_e * 1.03))

        if cand is not None and np.isfinite(cand_score):
            if cand_score < cur_score:
                accepted = True
            else:
                delta = cand_score - cur_score
                x = delta / T if T > 0.0 else 1e9
                if x < 60.0 and rng.random() < math.exp(-x):
                    accepted = True

            if accepted:
                p = cand
                cur_score = cand_score
                c = cand_c
                logp = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
                if cur_score < best_local_score:
                    best_local_score = cur_score
                    best_local_p = p.copy()
                    add_candidate(p)
                    no_improve = 0
                    if best_local_score < _TARGET:
                        print(f"Found fast score below target: {best_local_score:.6f}", flush=True)
                else:
                    no_improve += 1
                sigma = min(0.75, sigma * 1.02)
                T = max(1e-6, T * 0.995)
            else:
                no_improve += 1
                sigma = max(0.02, sigma * 0.975)
                eta = max(0.02, eta * 0.96)

        # If drifted far from best, reset to best with smaller scale.
        if no_improve > 400:
            p = best_local_p.copy()
            cur_score = best_local_score
            _, c = _fast_score_and_conv(p, n)
            logp = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
            sigma = max(0.04, sigma * 0.5)
            eta = max(0.04, eta * 0.5)
            T = max(1e-5, T * 0.5)
            no_improve = 0

        if it % 500 == 0:
            print(
                f"local it={it} current={cur_score:.6f} best_fast={best_local_score:.6f} "
                f"sigma={sigma:.4f} eta={eta:.4f}",
                flush=True
            )

    print("Local search finished. best fast =", best_local_score, flush=True)

    # Exact evaluation of a small number of top fast candidates.
    best_exact = float("inf")
    best_seq = None

    # Top fast candidates.
    candidates.sort()
    for sc, _, p_arr in candidates[:5]:
        seq = _to_valid_list(p_arr)
        val = _exact_score(seq)
        if np.isfinite(val) and val < best_exact:
            best_exact = val
            best_seq = seq

    # Safe fallback: uniform sequence (exact score = 2).
    uni_seq = [1.0] * n
    uni_val = _exact_score(uni_seq)
    if np.isfinite(uni_val) and uni_val < best_exact:
        best_exact = uni_val
        best_seq = uni_seq

    # Optional parent from external global, if available.
    parent = globals().get("height_sequence_1")
    if parent is not None:
        try:
            par = np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
            par = np.clip(par, 0.0, 1000.0)
            par = np.nan_to_num(par, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
            if par.size > 10:
                par_seq = [float(x) for x in par]
                par_val = _exact_score(par_seq)
                if np.isfinite(par_val) and par_val < best_exact:
                    best_exact = par_val
                    best_seq = par_seq
        except Exception:
            pass

    if best_seq is None or best_exact == float("inf"):
        best_seq = _to_valid_list(best_local_p)
        best_exact = _exact_score(best_seq)

    print("Final exact best =", best_exact, flush=True)
    return best_seq
```
